\title{Unipotent generators at parameter zero are trivial in the group $G(\mathfrak{m})$: a machine-checked proof}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\begin{abstract}
We present a formal verification of Lemma 3.1 from the source text on the group $G(\mathfrak{m})$. This group is defined by generators and relations that encode copies of $\mathrm{GL}_2$ indexed by the coefficients $c(j)$ of the modular function $J(q)$. The lemma states that the unipotent generators $X_{-1}(0)$, $Y_{-1}(0)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(0)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(0)$ are trivial. The formalization builds the full presentation as a presented group over the free group on the generating symbols. It takes the coefficient function as an arbitrary parameter $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ and needs no hypothesis on $c$. It proves all four parts, using only the additivity relations (Re:1a), (Re:1b), (Im:1a) and (Im:1b).
\end{abstract}

\section{Statement}

Let $\C$ denote the complex numbers and $\N$ the natural numbers. Let $c(j)$ denote the coefficient of $q^j$ in the modular function
\[
J(q)=\sum_{j\geq -1}c(j)q^j=\frac{1}{q}+196884\,q+21493760\,q^2+864299970\,q^3+\cdots,
\]
so that $c(-1)=1$, $c(0)=0$, $c(1)=196884$, and so on. Define the index set
\[
E=\{(\ell,j,k)\mid j\in\N,\ 1\leq k\leq c(j),\ 0\leq \ell<j\}
\]
and, for $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, the complex number
\[
c_{\ell j}:=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell).
\]
Let $\cX$ be the set of symbols
\[
\cX:=\{H_1(s),\,H_2(s),\,X_{-1}(u),\,Y_{-1}(u),\,X_{\ell,jk}(u),\,Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\mid s\in\C^\times,\ u\in\C,\ (\ell,j,k)\in E\},
\]
and let $F(\cX)$ be the free group on $\cX$. The symbols $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$, $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ are the \emph{unipotent generators}. The commutator is $(a,b):=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.

The set $\cR\subseteq F(\cX)$ and the auxiliary elements $\wt_{-1}(s)$ and $\wt_{\ell,jk}(s)$ are defined as follows. The relations hold for all $s,t\in\C^\times$, $u,v\in\C$ and $(\ell,j,k),(m,p,q)\in E$.
\begin{align*}
\tag{Re:0} \wt_{-1}(s)&:=X_{-1}(s)\,Y_{-1}(-s^{-1})\,X_{-1}(s),\qquad \wt_{-1}:=\wt_{-1}(1),\\
\tag{Im:0} \wt_{\ell,jk}(s)&:=X_{\ell,jk}(s)\,Y_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-s^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr)\,X_{\ell,jk}(s),\qquad \wt_{\ell,jk}:=\wt_{\ell,jk}(1),\\
\intertext{Relations that define a subgroup $\mathrm{GL}_2(-1)$:}
\tag{H:1a} H_1(s)H_1(t)&=H_1(st),\\
\tag{H:1b} H_2(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(st),\\
\tag{H:2} H_1(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(t)H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:1a} X_{-1}(u)X_{-1}(v)&=X_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:1b} Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v)&=Y_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:2} Y_{-1}(-t)X_{-1}(s)Y_{-1}(t)&=X_{-1}(-t^{-1})\,Y_{-1}(-t^{2}s)\,X_{-1}(t^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:3} \wt_{-1}(s)\wt_{-1}&=H_1(-s)H_2(-s^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:4a} \wt_{-1}X_{-1}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:4b} \wt_{-1}Y_{-1}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:5a} \wt_{-1}H_1(s)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=H_2(s),\\
\tag{Re:5b} \wt_{-1}H_2(s)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:6a} H_1(s)X_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Re:6b} H_2(s)X_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6c} H_1(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6d} H_2(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(su),\\
\intertext{Relations that define subgroups $\mathrm{GL}_2(\ell,j,k)$:}
\tag{Im:1a} X_{\ell,jk}(u)X_{\ell,jk}(v)&=X_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:1b} Y_{\ell,jk}(u)Y_{\ell,jk}(v)&=Y_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:2} Y_{\ell,jk}(-t)X_{\ell,jk}(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(t)&=X_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr)Y_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}t^2s)X_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr),\\
\tag{Im:3} \wt_{\ell,jk}\bigl(s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}\bigr)\wt_{\ell,jk}&=H_1\bigl((-s)^{j-\ell}\bigr)H_2\bigl((-s)^{\ell+1}\bigr),\\
\tag{Im:4a} \wt_{\ell,jk}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-u}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr),\\
\tag{Im:4b} \wt_{\ell,jk}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}u),\\
\tag{Im:5a} \wt_{\ell,jk}H_1(s^{j-\ell})\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_2(s^{-(\ell+1)}),\\
\tag{Im:5b} \wt_{\ell,jk}H_2(s^{\ell+1})\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_1(s^{-(j-\ell)}),\\
\tag{Im:6a} H_1(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{\ell+1}u),\\
\tag{Im:6b} H_2(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}u),\\
\tag{Im:6c} H_1(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(\ell+1)}u),\\
\tag{Im:6d} H_2(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(j-\ell)}u),\\
\intertext{Relations between the unipotent generators:}
\tag{U:1a} (X_{-1}(u),X_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1b} (Y_{-1}(u),Y_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1c} (Y_{-1}(u),X_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1d} (X_{-1}(u),Y_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:2} (X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{m,pq}(v))&=1\qquad\text{if $j\neq p$, or $k\neq q$, or $|\ell-m|>1$},\\
\tag{U:3a} (X_{1,2k}(u),Y_{0,2k}(v))&=X_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:3b} (X_{0,2k}(u),Y_{1,2k}(v))&=Y_{-1}(uv),\\
\intertext{Action of $\wt_{-1}$ on the unipotent generators:}
\tag{U:4a} \wt_{-1}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr),\\
\tag{U:4b} \wt_{-1}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr).
\end{align*}
The group $G(\mathfrak{m})$ is the group given by this presentation:
\[
G(\mathfrak{m})=\langle\cX\mid\cR\rangle=F(\cX)/N_\cR,
\]
where $N_\cR$ is the normal closure of $\cR$.

\begin{lemma}[Lemma 3.1 of the source]\label{lem:source}
In $G(\mathfrak{m})$ we have:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] $X_{-1}(0)=1$;
\item[(b)] $Y_{-1}(0)=1$;
\item[(c)] $X_{\ell,jk}(0)=1$ for all $(\ell,j,k)\in E$;
\item[(d)] $Y_{\ell,jk}(0)=1$ for all $(\ell,j,k)\in E$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

The source proves (a) by setting $u=v=0$ in (Re:1a), which gives $X_{-1}(0)^{-1}X_{-1}(0)X_{-1}(0)=1$, and hence $X_{-1}(0)=1$. Parts (b), (c) and (d) follow in the same way from (Re:1b), (Im:1a) and (Im:1b).

\section{Formalization}

Throughout, $c\colon\N\to\N$ is an arbitrary function. Here $\N=\{0,1,2,\dots\}$. It plays the role of the coefficients $c(j)$ of $J$ for $j\geq 0$. The value $c(-1)$ never enters the definition of $E$, so it is not represented. All objects below depend on $c$.

\begin{definition}[Index set]
$E_c$ is the set of triples $(\ell,j,k)\in\N^3$ with $1\leq k\leq c(j)$ and $\ell<j$.
\end{definition}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{IdxE}}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IdxE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{×}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{×}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

The condition $\ell<j$ forces $j\geq1$. Therefore it makes no difference whether the source's $\N$ contains $0$.

\begin{definition}[Generating symbols]
$\cX_c$ is the disjoint union of six families of symbols:
\begin{itemize}
\item $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\C^\times$;
\item $X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\C$;
\item $X_e(u)$ and $Y_e(u)$ for $e\in E_c$ and $u\in\C$.
\end{itemize}
For $e=(\ell,j,k)$ we also write $X_e(u)=X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_e(u)=Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[The constants $c_{\ell j}$]
For $\ell,j\in\N$,
\[
c_{\ell j}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)\in\C,
\]
where $j-1$ and $j-\ell$ are computed as truncated subtraction in $\N$.
\end{definition}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZca{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Nat}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{choose}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

For $(\ell,j,k)\in E_c$ we have $\ell<j$, so the truncated differences are the ordinary ones and $c_{\ell j}$ agrees with the source. Moreover $0\leq\ell\leq j-1$ gives $\binom{j-1}{\ell}\geq1$, so $c_{\ell j}\neq0$. In particular the divisions by $c_{\ell j}$ below are genuine divisions.

\begin{definition}[Index flip]
$\phi\colon E_c\to E_c$ is the map $(\ell,j,k)\mapsto(j-1-\ell,\,j,\,k)$. Since $\ell<j$, the integer $j-1-\ell$ lies in $[0,j)$, so the image lies in $E_c$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[The relators $\cR_c$]
$\cR_c\subseteq F(\cX_c)$ is defined as follows.
\begin{itemize}
\item Each equation $A=B$ among (H:1a)--(U:4b) contributes the relator $AB^{-1}$. This applies for all $s,t\in\C^\times$, $u,v\in\C$ and $e,f\in E_c$.
\item Each relation $(a,b)=1$ contributes $(a,b)$ itself.
\end{itemize}
The relators are written exactly as displayed in \cref{lem:source} and the relations preceding it, with $\wt_{-1}$ and $\wt_e$ as above. The following conventions apply.
\begin{itemize}
\item In (Im:5a), (Im:5b), (Im:6c) and (Im:6d), the negative powers $s^{-n}$ are written as $(s^n)^{-1}$.
\item In (Re:2) and (Im:2), the parameters $s,t$ range over $\C^\times$.
\item (U:1a) and (U:1b) are imposed for $e=(\ell,j,k)$ with $\ell+1=j$. (U:1c) and (U:1d) are imposed for $e$ with $\ell=0$.
\item (U:2) is imposed for $e=(\ell,j,k)$ and $f=(m,p,q)$ whenever $j\neq p$, or $k\neq q$, or $|\ell-m|>1$ (as integers).
\item (U:3a) is imposed for $e=(1,2,k)$ and $f=(0,2,k)$. (U:3b) is imposed for $e=(0,2,k)$ and $f=(1,2,k)$.
\item (U:4a) and (U:4b) use the flipped index $\phi(e)$ and the sign $(-1)^{j-\ell-1}$. Here $j-\ell-1\geq0$ for $e\in E_c$.
\end{itemize}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[The group]
$G_c=F(\cX_c)/N_{\cR_c}$, where $N_{\cR_c}$ is the normal closure of $\cR_c$. We write $\pi\colon F(\cX_c)\to G_c$ for the quotient homomorphism.
\end{definition}

The target statement asserts the following for every function $c\colon\N\to\N$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] $\pi(X_{-1}(0))=1$ in $G_c$;
\item[(b)] $\pi(Y_{-1}(0))=1$;
\item[(c)] for every $e\in E_c$, $\pi(X_e(0))=1$;
\item[(d)] for every $e\in E_c$, $\pi(Y_e(0))=1$.
\end{enumerate}

This is \cref{lem:source}, with "$=1$ in $G(\mathfrak{m})$" read as "the image under the quotient map is the identity". The function $c$ is a free parameter, and no hypothesis on it is assumed; in particular $c(2)\geq1$ is not needed here. Specializing $c$ to the coefficients $c(j)$, $j\geq0$, of $J$ recovers the group $G(\mathfrak{m})$ of the source, so the formal statement contains the source lemma.

\section{Proof}

\subsection{Overview}
The source proof sets $u=v=0$ in (Re:1a) and cancels. The formal proof does the same, organized as follows.
\begin{itemize}
\item It isolates the cancellation step as an abstract fact (\cref{lem:add_zero_group_lemma}): any map $f\colon\C\to G$ into a group with $f(u+v)=f(u)f(v)$ satisfies $f(0)=1$.
\item It derives this additivity for $u\mapsto\pi(X_{-1}(u))$, $u\mapsto\pi(Y_{-1}(u))$, $u\mapsto\pi(X_e(u))$ and $u\mapsto\pi(Y_e(u))$. These are \cref{lem:mk_xm_add,lem:mk_ym_add,lem:mk_xx_add,lem:mk_yy_add}, which use (Re:1a), (Re:1b), (Im:1a) and (Im:1b) respectively.
\end{itemize}
The only deviations from the source are presentational. Parts (b)--(d), which the source leaves to the reader, are written out. The additivity is first established for all $u,v$ and only afterwards specialized to $u=v=0$. No other relation of $\cR_c$ is used.

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{add_zero_group_lemma}]\label{lem:add_zero_group_lemma}
Let $G$ be a group and let $f\colon\C\to G$ be any map such that $f(u+v)=f(u)f(v)$ for all $u,v\in\C$. Then $f(0)=1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Taking $u=v=0$ gives $f(0)=f(0+0)=f(0)f(0)$. Hence
\[
f(0)=f(0)\bigl(f(0)f(0)^{-1}\bigr)=\bigl(f(0)f(0)\bigr)f(0)^{-1}=f(0)f(0)^{-1}=1.\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{mk_xm_add}]\label{lem:mk_xm_add}
Let $c\colon\N\to\N$ and $u,v\in\C$. Then in $G_c$
\[
\pi(X_{-1}(u+v))=\pi(X_{-1}(u))\,\pi(X_{-1}(v)).
\]
\end{lemma}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{h1}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h2}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{eq\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}mul\PYZus{}inv\PYZus{}eq\PYZus{}one}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h2}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{symm}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
By (Re:1a), $r=X_{-1}(u)X_{-1}(v)\,X_{-1}(u+v)^{-1}\in\cR_c$, so $\pi(r)=1$. Since $\pi$ is a homomorphism,
\[
\pi(X_{-1}(u))\,\pi(X_{-1}(v))\,\pi(X_{-1}(u+v))^{-1}=1.
\]
Multiplying on the right by $\pi(X_{-1}(u+v))$ gives $\pi(X_{-1}(u))\pi(X_{-1}(v))=\pi(X_{-1}(u+v))$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{mk_ym_add}]\label{lem:mk_ym_add}
Let $c\colon\N\to\N$ and $u,v\in\C$. Then in $G_c$
\[
\pi(Y_{-1}(u+v))=\pi(Y_{-1}(u))\,\pi(Y_{-1}(v)).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By (Re:1b), $Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v)\,Y_{-1}(u+v)^{-1}\in\cR_c$, so its image under $\pi$ is $1$. That is, $\pi(Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v))\,\pi(Y_{-1}(u+v))^{-1}=1$. Multiplying on the right by $\pi(Y_{-1}(u+v))$ gives $\pi(Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v))=\pi(Y_{-1}(u+v))$. Finally, $\pi(Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v))=\pi(Y_{-1}(u))\pi(Y_{-1}(v))$ because $\pi$ is a homomorphism.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{mk_xx_add}]\label{lem:mk_xx_add}
Let $c\colon\N\to\N$, $e\in E_c$ and $u,v\in\C$. Then in $G_c$
\[
\pi(X_e(u+v))=\pi(X_e(u))\,\pi(X_e(v)).
\]
\end{lemma}

\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
By (Im:1a) for the index $e$, $\bigl(X_e(u)X_e(v)\bigr)X_e(u+v)^{-1}\in\cR_c$. If $ab^{-1}\in\cR_c$, then $\pi(a)=\pi(b)$. Hence $\pi(X_e(u)X_e(v))=\pi(X_e(u+v))$. Since $\pi$ is a homomorphism, the left-hand side equals $\pi(X_e(u))\pi(X_e(v))$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{mk_yy_add}]\label{lem:mk_yy_add}
Let $c\colon\N\to\N$, $e\in E_c$ and $u,v\in\C$. Then in $G_c$
\[
\pi(Y_e(u+v))=\pi(Y_e(u))\,\pi(Y_e(v)).
\]
\end{lemma}

\end{leanblock}

Fix $c\colon\N\to\N$. Each part is an application of \cref{lem:add_zero_group_lemma} with $G=G_c$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Take $f(u)=\pi(X_{-1}(u))$. It is additive by \cref{lem:mk_xm_add}, so $\pi(X_{-1}(0))=1$.
\item[(b)] Take $f(u)=\pi(Y_{-1}(u))$. It is additive by \cref{lem:mk_ym_add}, so $\pi(Y_{-1}(0))=1$.
\item[(c)] Given $e\in E_c$, take $f(u)=\pi(X_e(u))$. It is additive by \cref{lem:mk_xx_add}, so $\pi(X_e(0))=1$.
\item[(d)] Given $e\in E_c$, take $f(u)=\pi(Y_e(u))$. It is additive by \cref{lem:mk_yy_add}, so $\pi(Y_e(0))=1$.
\end{enumerate}
This proves all four parts of the target statement. Because $c$ is arbitrary, it proves \cref{lem:source} in particular. \qed

\section{Independent review}

A referee model (claude-opus-5-5), which took no part in the formalization or the proof, compared the verified Lean file with the source. Verdict: \textbf{FAITHFUL}.

\paragraph{Definitions.} I compared each definition with the source.
\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{IdxE} encodes $E$ as triples $(\ell,j,k)$ of naturals with $1 \le k \le c(j)$ and $\ell < j$. Here $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ is a parameter, as the formalization note allows. The case $j=0$ gives no elements because $\ell<0$ is impossible, so the convention for $\mathbb{N}$ does not matter.
\item \leanname{Gen} encodes $\mathcal{X}$: $H_1,H_2$ on $\mathbb{C}^\times$, $X_{-1},Y_{-1}$ on $\mathbb{C}$, and $X_{\ell,jk},Y_{\ell,jk}$ on $E\times\mathbb{C}$.
\item \leanname{cc} is $(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)$. The truncated subtractions are harmless because $\ell<j$ on $E$. The value is nonzero on $E$, so the Lean convention for division by zero never arises.
\item \leanname{wm}, \leanname{wm1}, \leanname{ww} and \leanname{ww1} match (Re:0) and (Im:0).
\item The helper \leanname{comm'} is $aba^{-1}b^{-1}$, the source convention. The helper \leanname{eqr} encodes $a=b$ as $ab^{-1}$, and \leanname{conj'} is $aba^{-1}$.
\item \leanname{GRel}: I checked each constructor against its tag. The H, Re and Im relations agree, including the exponents in (Im:3), (Im:5) and (Im:6); for example $s^{-(\ell+1)}$ is written as $(s^{\ell+1})^{-1}$.
\item The U relations also agree.
  \begin{itemize}
  \item (U:1a,b) use $\ell+1=j$, and (U:1c,d) use $\ell=0$.
  \item (U:2) uses the disjunction $j\ne p$, $k\ne q$, or $|\ell-m|>1$.
  \item (U:3a,b) use the correct indices $(1,2,k)$ and $(0,2,k)$.
  \item (U:4a,b) use \leanname{flipE}, which sends $\ell$ to $j-1-\ell$, with sign $(-1)^{j-\ell-1}$.
  \end{itemize}
\item \leanname{Gm} is the presented group $F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}}$.
\end{itemize}
I found no discrepancy.

\paragraph{Statement.} The theorem is quantified over every $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ and has no further hypotheses. It asserts that the images in \leanname{Gm} of the following are all $1$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $X_{-1}(0)$ and $Y_{-1}(0)$;
\item $X_{\ell,jk}(0)$ for every $e\in E$;
\item $Y_{\ell,jk}(0)$ for every $e\in E$.
\end{itemize}
These are exactly parts (a) through (d).

To recover the source statement, instantiate $c$ with the coefficients of $J$ restricted to $j\in\mathbb{N}$; these are natural numbers. The value $c(-1)$ never enters $E$.

The conclusion is not vacuous. Parts (a) and (b) are unconditional, and parts (c) and (d) range over a nonempty $E$ for the true $c$. The relation set also has no degenerate encoding.

\paragraph{Proof.} \begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{add_zero_group_lemma} shows that if $f(u+v)=f(u)f(v)$ in a group, then $f(0)=1$. It does this by deriving $f(0)=f(0)f(0)$ and cancelling, which is exactly the source argument with $u=v=0$.
\item \leanname{mk_xm_add}, \leanname{mk_ym_add}, \leanname{mk_xx_add} and \leanname{mk_yy_add} turn the relations (Re:1a), (Re:1b), (Im:1a) and (Im:1b), each a member of the relation set, into multiplicativity of the image in \leanname{Gm}. They use \leanname{PresentedGroup.one_of_mem} or \leanname{PresentedGroup.mk_eq_mk_of_mul_inv_mem}.
\item \leanname{solution} combines these four lemmas with \leanname{add_zero_group_lemma}.
\end{itemize}
The proof follows the source proof, and its note covering parts (b) to (d). No auxiliary lemma is weaker or stronger than the step it represents.

\paragraph{Concerns.} None
